| 8f.122 | **#94 SHIPPED - THE BATTLE SCREEN, ROUND THREE: TWELVE POINTS OFF AN ANNOTATED SCREENSHOT.** *(User, 2026-08-11, hours after #91 landed: a marked-up battle shot plus twelve lines, from «Шов дисингейдж онли вен енгейдж» through «По умолчанию батл лог свернутый настолько, что в нем не видно деталей».)* ⛔ **THE SHAPE OF THE LIST IS THE FINDING, AND IT IS NOT WHAT #91 EXPECTED.** #91 moved every box on this screen a day earlier and the follow-up did **not** ask for anything to move back: nine of the twelve are *finish* on the frame that shipped, and **the three that are new behaviour are all the same complaint - the board was answering questions with a hover or with nothing.** ⚑ **THE THREE WORTH THE ENTRY.** **(1) A SELECTED SKILL DRAWS ITS OWN REACH.** The board has always lit the TARGETS an act can hit, which quietly refuses to answer *how far does this thing go*: with nobody standing in range a bow, a spear and a fist are the same picture - an empty board - so the one number separating three classes lived on a hover at the moment of choosing between them. ⚠ **AND THE ORDERING IS THE WHOLE FEATURE**: selecting an attack does not clear `B.reach`, so the walk preview from the MOVE that was standing a moment ago is still painted, and a footprint written before it was overpainted teal on every hex the body could also walk to - **measured, a bow at range 5 kept 22 of its 59 hexes and A KICK AT REACH 1 KEPT NONE**, which is precisely the case the request names (*«для мили атаки токо вокруг перса»*). Written after it: bow 59/59, kick 5/5, MOVE 0 with its 40 walk badges untouched. **(2) A BODY CROSSES THE GROUND INSTEAD OF ARRIVING ON IT.** Every walk was a teleport between two renders; on your own turn that is flat, on **theirs it deletes information you are entitled to** - which way something came, and therefore where it can be next round. #87 had already given the three acts that really do teleport their GHOST, and a great beast already walked her head hex by hex in the DATA so her tail could follow: ordinary movement was the one that still blinked. ⚑ **ONE DOOR, AND IT IS `render()`** - `walkTo()` writes the hexes and PARKS the picture, render() plays whatever is parked as its last act, so aiTurn, autoStep, the pet, the fallback, the kite and the player's own click all inherited it by calling one function instead of assigning two fields, with **no change to either brain's control flow**. ⚠ **AND IT IS PURE DECORATION ON PURPOSE**: the hexes are committed before a frame is drawn and no timer of its own carries a `B.busy` release, which is the exact shape of B02, the AUTO deadlock - drop every timer in it and the worst case is a token hidden for one render. **(3) THE POOLS ARE SECTIONED** (the user's ARC Raiders shield note, and the spec is his sentence *"the more general pool the smaller each section"*). One unbroken bar can only say a FRACTION, and 60% of a ratkin and 60% of the Fen-Mother are the same picture; the question at the table is never *what fraction*, it is *how many more blows*. `SEG(max)` is sublinear and capped (6 armour → 3 fat sections, 45 hitpoints → 6, a boss at 120 → 8), it takes the MAX and never the current value so the sections stay nailed while the pool drains, and it goes through `setBar()` - the same door the width does - so the two cannot come apart. **It is on the token bars too, which is where it earns its keep: the plaque only ever describes one person and the board's question is which of six to swing at.** ⚑ **DISENGAGE LEAVES THE ROW, AND THE REFUSAL WENT IN `canUse` RATHER THAN THE RENDERER** - the one door both AI brains, AUTO, the hotkeys and the click already ask, so the card filter is a pure question about what is usable with nothing to keep in step. **It buys exactly one thing, a step that pays no parting swing, so with nobody engaging it was offering a worse version of the card beside it** on every sheet in every fight. ⚠ **AND THE HOTKEYS HAD TO FOLLOW IT**: DISENGAGE is *not* last on every sheet - a gilled or bloom-handed body has SINK BELOW or ROOTING GRASP pushed after it - so hiding it in place would have left a hole in the numbering **for exactly the people with the most to read**. `B.cards` maps visible slot → real index, rebuilt every render, read by the keydown handler. The rest as asked: **log top-left and six whole lines instead of three-and-a-half** (46→92px; the corner it vacates is the one END TURN needed) · **END TURN 308x40 full blood → 190x34 dark blood that lights to the old colour under the hand**, with **⏱ PACE and ⚑ WITHDRAW brought down beside it** off a 1280px diagonal, the camera stops taking the top-right corner back · **the order rail down under the cards** · **nickname on the name's line**, shrink factor 2 so the nick gives way first · **actions as crystals** - ⚠ their third form, and not a circle: #88 cut the pips because they were a THIRD way of saying one fact beside a numeral and a word, and the numeral went with them · **`15%` on dodge** and CHANCE TO HIT → TO HIT · **the MOVE card carries `4 HEXES` on its face**, off the same `moveBudget()` call as the plaque. ⚑ **THE BUG THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT, AND IT IS README §6'S SPECIFICITY TRAP WEARING A NEW COAT: A CLASS NAME THAT ALREADY BELONGED TO SOMETHING ELSE.** `<span class="adm hex">` picked up `.hex`, **the board's own hex button** - width 37, **height 42**, display:flex - and inside a flex-column card that gave one span a 42px box and **squeezed the skill's NAME down to 3px**. Nothing threw, nothing looked broken in the rule, and it was found only by printing every child's height. Renamed `.stride`. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` **three times, all eight fights resolve every time, `__errs` empty and `DROPPED` 0** (the B02 canary) · **chrome overlap sweep 0 at FULL, FIELD and CLOSE**, with the log open, the legend open, an eleven-head rail and UNDO showing - ten boxes each pass · the footprint counted exactly against the geometry on three acts · **every class in the roster swept for a clipped card name, a spilling damage line and a spilling name row: clean** · a real `aiTurn` run parked two chieftain walks of 5 and 4 tiles along the true path · the walker clone removes itself and the token comes back · the user's save byte-identical, `SIM.on` true throughout. **PICTURES: `shots/94_css_bench.html`** - the shipping CSS over a static stage **before the prototype was touched**, two frames (rail under the cards, rail above), each self-auditing: 0 overlaps, 0 offstage, 0 clipped names - **and `shots/94_after.html`**, the built screen serialized live with the bow raised and its whole range on the ground. ⚠ **THE BOARD LOST 2% TO THE TALLER LOG** (85.5% → 83.5%): that is the price of the sixth line and it is named rather than hidden. ⚠ **OPEN: the rail's placement is the user's ruling** - *"maybe place under the skills?"* was a question, it is built literally, and FRAME B in the bench is a one-line switch. ⚠ **OPEN, and the same caveat #91 shipped with: nobody has played it with a hand on a mouse** - the hidden pane composites no frames, so the walk's *pace* (`WALK_HEX`, 95ms a tile before the x1.75 clock) is a judgement nobody here can make. ⚠ **The phone was again unexercisable**, and the log moving to the top left is the part that wants a portrait look. **#94 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.123 | **#95 SHIPPED - THE BATTLE SCREEN, ROUND FOUR: TEN POINTS, AND TWO OF THEM CORRECT #94.** *(User, 2026-08-11, a second annotated battle shot the same day.)* ⛔ **THE ENTRY'S VALUE IS THAT THE USER CAUGHT TWO THINGS #94 SHIPPED WRONG, AND BOTH WERE THE SAME MISTAKE: A CHANGE THAT LOOKED LIKE AN ANSWER AND ANSWERED NOTHING.** ⚑ **(1) A SECTION HAS TO BE A FIXED QUANTITY OR IT IS DECORATION.** #94 built the sectioned pools with a **sublinear, capped COUNT** - `max(3,min(8,round(max/8)))` - so a notch meant "about a blow" on a ratkin and something quite different on a boss. It made a nicer bar and **you still could not read anything off it**. The user's correction is the whole design: *"each section of hitpoint respresent same ammount of health for all the units... I feel one section more 15 health and 1 secion of armor is 10 armor."* **Count the notches, multiply, and you know the pool without hovering anybody** - which is the only thing a sectioned bar is FOR, and it kills the cap. Measured against every unit in the act: hitpoints run **18 to 112** (1 to 7 sections) and armour **3 to 66** (1 to 7), so the whole roster and every ordinary foe draw cleanly at 3.7px a section on a 26px token bar. ⚠ **THE TWO BOSSES BREAK THAT BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE** - the Fen-Mother is **470/170** (31 and 17 sections) and the Thing in Armour **342/162** - and **the answer is that their bar gets LONGER rather than finer** (`barW`, 26px floor rising to 54px), because a cap would have broken the promise at exactly the two bodies where "how much is left" is the whole fight, while 31 hairlines in 26px would draw a full boss as an empty bar. **The length now says "this pool is enormous" before a notch is counted, and nothing under 8 sections moves at all, so the board is unchanged for every other body in the game.** ⚑ **(2) A DUPLICATE IS BORN THE DAY IT SHIPS.** #94 put the hex count on the MOVE card's face **and left the plaque's HEXES cell standing**, which is the second half of the original 2026-08-10 report ("too many numbers everywhere") re-committed in the entry that was answering it. The card is where the decision is made, so the card keeps it; the plaque is three cells now. **Same shape, same day, third time: `moveBudget()` is one call and it wanted one reader.** ⚑ **THE TURN ORDER WENT LEFT AND VERTICAL, AND THE REASON THE BOTTOM WAS WRONG IS WORTH THE ROW:** #94 put the rail under the cards on the user's own *"maybe place under the skills?"*, and a queue read left-to-right **competes with the CARD ROW for the same band and the same reading direction** - two different lists in one strip. Down the left edge it is a column of faces beside a board. ⚠ **AND IT IS GIVEN A BAND, NOT A CENTRE.** Anchored to `top:50%` it was clear of the log and the plaque only at the head counts that happened to be tested: **eleven heads centre to 158-562 and the plaque's real top is 561**, so it collided on the first eleven-body board. Pinned top AND bottom it is a 490px band whatever is in it, 13 heads (the widest legal board) is 478px, and any future overflow is clipped rather than laid on the plaque. ⚑ **THE CAPSULE LOST WHOSE TURN IT IS AND THE USER'S REASONING IS EXACTLY RIGHT:** with the rail on the left, the gold ring IS whose turn it is, the plaque under it is who they are, and the card row appearing or not is whether it is yours - **three answers to one question, and the one at the top of the screen was reading a name off a panel the player is already looking at.** It also shrank the capsule from 258px to **157px**, which is a third of the top-centre crop given back. ⚑ **`? RULES` WAS NEVER HIDDEN, IT WAS COVERED, WHICH IS THE WORSE FAILURE:** it has lived at `left:10 bottom:10` since before the battle screen was full-bleed, and #91 put the plaque on that exact corner at z-index 40 against its 20 - **the control still existed, still worked, and could not be found.** It is **reparented into the log box as a footer rather than repositioned**, because the log's height changes (shut, or 300px open) and anything placed under it by coordinate is right in one state and wrong in the other. ⚑ **AND THE COLLISION SWEEP LEARNED SOMETHING: MEASURE THE FURNITURE, NOT ONLY THE FURNITURE YOU BUILT.** The user reported ⚙ TEST eating the `CLOSE` camera stop (TEST measures **77px**, not the 62 it looks, so the bar sits at right:96); the same sweep, widened to include global chrome for the first time, found **♪ sound sitting on top of END TURN** - unreported, shipped in #94, and invisible to a sweep that only looked at ids beginning with `b`. **Both overlaps were between a battle box and a piece of chrome older than the screen.** ⚑ **FULL WAS CROPPING THE TOP RANK AND "EXACTLY FITS" WAS THE BUG.** ×1.73 was defined as *the whole board exactly fits*, height-exact - and **a body is drawn bottom-anchored in a hex it stands ~8px proud of**, so at 1.73 the top row lost **14px of head** to `overflow:hidden` on a stop whose entire promise is that you can see everything. ×1.58 now, and ⚠ **the slack is split UNEVENLY on purpose - 49px above, 13 below** - because only the top end can crop a body and only the top end has the counts capsule floating over it: a top-rank head lands at y37 against a capsule ending at 34. Still 1.22x the pre-#91 FULL. Also: **the log starts shut at the top of every fight** (#94 read *"cannot see details"* as "not deep enough" and made it six lines; the answer was that **a log you did not ask for is furniture whatever depth it is**), and **"LEVEL 1" became "Lv 1"** on a line holding a class, a level and a race inside 194px. ⚠ **THE BUILD BUG, AND IT COST TWENTY MINUTES:** a comment inserted above `#bLogWrap` **closed a comment that was already closed**, leaving a stray `*/` and turning the next rule into garbage - the whole log box silently fell back to `position:static`, 720x577, and the tell was not an error but a measured box that made no sense. **CSS fails silently in this file and only measurement finds it**, which is the same lesson as #94's `.hex` class collision one day earlier. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, `__errs` empty, `DROPPED` 0 · **overlap sweep now covers twelve boxes including ⚙ TEST, ♪ and ? RULES follows the log shut and open, opens the rules screen, does not toggle the accordion, and goes home to `#stage` on every other screen · the log re-shuts on a new fight · the user's save untouched. **PICTURE: `shots/95_after.html`**, the Fen-Mother's board serialized live. ⚠ **OPEN: nobody has played it with a hand on a mouse** - unchanged from #91 and #94, and it matters most for FULL, which is now a judgement about how big a body should look. ⚠ **Everything at FULL is 9% smaller than yesterday**; the number is one constant if that is too much. **#95 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.124 | **#96 SHIPPED - WHOSE BODY IS THAT: THE RAIL HOVER, THE SIDE COLOURS, AND STATUSES ON THE HEAD.** *(User, 2026-08-11, third list of the day.)* ⚑ **THE SENTENCE AFTER THE COLOUR REQUEST IS THE REQUEST, AND IT IS THE WHOLE CLARITY PASS IN ONE LINE:** *"So even if you don`t understed design figure, you can understend that color."* The side has always been readable - the sprite is tinted by side, the order rail borders by side, the threat edges by side - and **every one of those cues asks the player to already know what a ratkin looks like**, which is exactly what the 2026-08-10 report said they do not. **A bar is the one mark on a token that is the same shape on every body in the game**, so it is the only place a side can be stated in a way that needs no prior knowledge at all. **Blue yours · red theirs · gold fighting beside you.** ⚠ **THE HUE IS THE SIDE AND THE LIGHTNESS IS THE WOUND, AND BOTH HAD TO SURVIVE** - red-for-hurt was the old scheme's entire job, so each side keeps a three-step ramp inside its own family and brightens as it drops. ⚠ **THE ARMOUR BAR STAYS STEEL ON EVERYBODY**: it is the shell rather than the person, it sits directly above the health bar, and colouring both would be two marks saying one thing and neither saying its own. ⚠ **The pet is scored as an ALLY, not as yours** - the company's, but not on the roster and not commandable; `hpSide()` is the one place that decides, and the clash's allied ratkin (side `you`, `ally:true`) come out gold, which was the case worth checking because they are the only bodies in the act on your side and not in your company. ⚑ **THE RAIL HOVER CLOSES A HOLE #91 OPENED ON PURPOSE.** The order rail is faces with **no names and no initiative numbers** - correct, and it left the rail unable to answer the one question a face raises, *which of the eleven bodies out there is that*. Resting on a head now rings the body in gold on the ground and glows its sprite. **It is stored on `B.spot` and repainted from `render()`, the same shape as `B.peek`**, so a render that lands while the cursor is resting does not lose it; and it lights `hexesOf(u)` rather than one hex, because a great beast is two of them and lighting only her head points at half a body. ⚠ **THE RING IS A `::before` ON `.hedge` AND THAT IS FORCED, NOT PREFERRED**: `.hedge` carries the hexagon clip-path, so a box-shadow outside it is clipped away entirely and one inside it is a rectangle with the corners cut off - **and its `background` is written INLINE by render() from `edge`, so no class can ever win against it.** A pseudo-element under its own clip-path is the only layer there that is both hexagonal and free. ⚑ **AND THE STATUS POINT WAS ALREADY BUILT, WHICH IS THE FINDING.** The user hedged it - *"if you haven`t done it"* - and it had been done: every status and aura, from one register, capped at four, with a full hover. **They were stacked down the LEFT EDGE of the hex at 10px**, which is the busiest strip on a token (the weapon badge, the two formation marks and the sprite's own shoulder all live there), **so a real feature read as an absent one.** A row above the head is the only empty band on a 37px hex, and the things already up there own single points rather than the row (the crest dead centre at -2, the mood face hard right at -2). ⚠ **IT CLEARS THE MOOD FACE BY MEASUREMENT AND NOT BY EYE**: the face's line box is ~11px so it really occupies -2 to 9, and the band is 11px at -15, which is -15 to -4 - verified live with the rung forced down so the face was actually drawn. ⛔ **AND THE BUILD MADE THE SAME MISTAKE AS #95, IN THE SAME HOUR, WHICH IS WHY IT IS NOW A CHECK AND NOT A RESOLUTION: A COMMENT ADDED ABOVE A RULE CLOSED A COMMENT THAT WAS ALREADY CLOSED.** The stray `*/` turned `.stbar` into garbage and the tell was a status band measuring 47px tall and 16px wide on a 33px hex - **no error, no console warning, just a box whose numbers made no sense.** Third silent-CSS failure in two days (#94's `.hex` class collision, #95's first stray `*/`). **The session now runs a CSS AUDIT before anything else: every selector it wrote is asserted to resolve, by computed style for the live boxes and by scanning `document.styleSheets[0].cssRules` for the ones that only exist on generated nodes.** That check is what confirmed the fix rather than a re-read. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** the CSS audit passes on all thirteen selectors · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, `__errs` empty, `DROPPED` 0 · the rail hover lights the right hex, **survives a render**, and clears on leave; the Fen-Mother lights both `12,3` and `13,3` · the three families measured off the live tokens on a board carrying all of them (`you` 47,111,158 · `ally` 154,122,44 · `foe` 163,59,52) and the plaque matching · the status band measured at -15 to -4 against a visible mood face at -2 to 9, three glyphs, 45px on a 33px `.hin` · the user's save untouched. **PICTURE: `shots/96_after.html`** - one frame carrying all three, with a foe's head under the cursor. **#96 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.126 | **#98 SHIPPED - THE BATTLE SCREEN, ROUND FIVE, AND A GUARD AGAINST THE BUG THAT KEEPS SHIPPING.** *(User, 2026-08-11: a third annotated battle shot, seven points, plus "I don`t want any of these in backlog - i want it build and commit".)* ⚑ **THE RAIL BECOMES A QUEUE, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY'S ONE REAL DESIGN CHANGE.** It held the whole initiative list with a ring on the current body, so eleven faces answered *"who is in this fight"* - which the board already answers at a glance - and the half a player actually needs, **who is still to come**, had to be worked out by counting round the ring. Walked from `B.idx` forward and wrapped, it now shows whoever is acting, then whoever is left, then a **↻ divider naming the next round**, then that round's order. Bodies that have had their turn leave until the divider brings them back. Heads went **30x34 to 40x46** because at 22px of sprite a ratkin and a poacher were the same coloured smudge, and `RAIL_MAX` is 9, derived from the 490px band rather than guessed. ⚑ **AND THE DAMAGE ON A HEX BECAME THE WHOLE BLOW, WHICH REVERSES A DELIBERATE OLDER DECISION.** It printed the HITPOINT half only, on the written argument that adding the two together *"would read as a promise the target does not keep"* - so an arming sword against 44 armour read `7-9`. The user's correction is better and the reason is about WHEN the number is read: it is read while **choosing between two cards**, and at that moment `7-9` tells you about the target's plate, which the armour bar already says, and nothing at all about whether the sword or the kick is the harder swing. `dmgPreview` gained a `full` field carrying every multiplier that is about the SWING (weapon, bonus, arc, global trim, spell power) and none that are about the shell; measured live, a bow reading `14-21` on its card prices at **16-23** on the hex against the old `7-9`. **The odds dropped 11px to 9.5px in the same edit and that is not cosmetic** - two numbers on one hex want a hierarchy, and with the damage now the larger figure the percentage had to yield the emphasis it was holding; the split is still one hover away on the readout card, which is where a breakdown belongs. ⚑ **THE FLAG COLLISION WAS REAL AND HAD BEEN THERE FROM THE START.** *(User: "for a leader more icon star, then flag. we use flag for a morale.")* 🏳 above a head means BROKEN AND RUNNING and ⚑ above a head meant leader: two flags on one anchor, one of them the worst news in the game - and the code proved it, because the routed branch and the leader branch are an `if/else` on the same pixel, so a routed captain silently swapped one flag for the other. ★ now, on the token, the plaque, the readout card and the class line. **The rest as asked:** FULL **1.58 to 1.64** (49px of headroom to 31, 13 below to 7) ⚠ **and half of that complaint was never the camera** - #95 moved the order rail off the bottom edge and left `#bActions` parked at `bottom:48`, so 38px of the "space from the bottom" was a hole where the rail used to be, and the cards are back on the floor · **one right-hand column** read from the floor up, FEEDBACK · END TURN · ♪ ⏱ ⚑ · UNDO, on a 32/38/32 rhythm instead of four things floating at their own heights · **the log is a 150px button shut and 360x426 open** (a 308px bar holding two words was a panel pretending to be shut) · **the reaction bar folds to the word FEEDBACK**, 68x19, ⚠ deliberately NOT remembered across launches because that would be a settings-band write for a UI-band fact and a player who folds it once has said "not now", not "never". ⛔ **THE ENTRY'S REAL LESSON IS A BUILD BUG THAT HAS NOW SHIPPED TWICE IN THREE DAYS AND IS FINALLY GUARDED.** A stray comment terminator inside a CSS comment ends it early and **every rule after that point is silently discarded**: nothing throws, the console stays clean, and the page renders with a hole that reads as a design opinion. #95 dropped the whole battle-log box to `position:static` this way; #98 dropped **everything from the icon row to the foot of the sheet**, including both hex readouts, and the tell was `getComputedStyle` returning **16px** - the browser default - for a rule that plainly existed in the source. **There is now a `#cssTail` sentinel as the last rule in the sheet and a boot check that asks the PARSER whether it reached it**, setting `window.__cssTail` and printing a plain-English console line when it did not. ⚠ **And the guard's own first draft quoted the offending two characters inside its own comment, closed itself early, and took the entire script down with `SyntaxError: Unexpected identifier 'window'`** - which is either funny or the best possible argument for having written it. The rule that came out of it: **do not name the sequence in a comment, say "comment terminator".** **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `window.__cssTail` **true** · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, `__errs` empty, `DROPPED` 0 · **twelve-box overlap sweep clean at FULL, FIELD and CLOSE, with the log open and with FEEDBACK open** · the queue verified mid-round (*slinger · warp-sniffer · ogre · ↻ II · Ilka · chieftain · Vesna…*, with the eight who had acted absent before the divider and present after it) · the log measured 150 shut and 360x426 open **with the transition disabled**, because a `getBoundingClientRect` mid-glide reported 150 and would have read as a broken rule · FULL's margins computed rather than measured, for the same reason · the two crests read ★ · the user's save untouched. **PICTURE: `shots/98_after.html`.** ⚠ **OPEN: nobody has played it with a hand on a mouse**, unchanged since #91 - and it now matters for the queue, whose whole value is whether "who is next" is readable at a glance while you are deciding. ⚠ **The number between this entry and the last is a claimed-but-unbuilt hole**: a parallel session reserved it for this same list, stalled before its first edit, and its lock was taken over on the user's explicit instruction. **#98 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.127 | **#99 SHIPPED - THE ARCHER THAT WILL NOT SHOOT. A SHOOTER PICKED ITS DISTANCE AND NEVER ITS LINE.** *(User, 2026-08-11: "found a bag - that sometimes archer don`t shoot (at least in autobattle)".)* ⚑ **BOTH movement scorers chose a shooter's hex on DISTANCE ALONE.** `autoStep` scored the band (`Math.abs(dd-Math.min(rng-1,3))*7`), `aiTurn` scored `doc.band`, and neither had ever asked whether an arrow could LEAVE the hex being scored. So the archer walked to a textbook shooting position behind its own shieldwall or behind an oak, found the lane BLOCKED, and did the only thing left: stood there. Next turn it scored the same hexes by the same rule and stood there again. **The archer was not refusing to shoot, it was standing where shooting is impossible, and nothing in either brain had ever asked.** ⚑ **THE FIX IS ONE PARAMETER AND ONE TABLE.** `losState(a,d,from)` takes an optional eye, so the question the board has always asked (*can I shoot from here*) becomes the one a shooter must ask before it walks (*could I shoot from THERE*); `laneCost()` prices the answer in **hexes of walking**, the currency both scorers already use, so one table reads correctly in both without either being re-tuned: **CLEAR/SCREENED 0 · COVER 2 · OBSTRUCTED 5 · BLOCKED 12**. Twelve is more than the whole band range on purpose - a shooter leaves its perfect distance rather than stand where it cannot shoot - and deliberately does NOT swamp exposure (~36 for a thin skin), so it will take a more dangerous hex to have a shot but will not walk into the middle of them for one. ⚠ **THE `from` HEX GOES IN THE `_losCache` KEY.** The cache is keyed on the pair of bodies; a hypothetical eye is a different question about the same pair, and without the suffix the first hex scored answers for every hex after it - a scorer asking about forty hexes gets one verdict forty times. Not a slow bug, a silent one. ⚠ **THE SCREENED TEST HAD TO MOVE TOO** (`udist(eye,s)`, `sectorOf(...,eye)`): left reading `a` it says *screened* off a shieldman standing beside the archer's CURRENT hex while scoring a hex six steps away. ⛔ **THE MEASUREMENT NEARLY SHIPPED THE WRONG VERDICT, TWICE, AND THE INSTRUMENT WAS THE BUG BOTH TIMES.** First batch (n=64) said +0.16 shots per battle; second (n=128) said **-0.20**, and a 4-point weight sweep (0/4/8/12) came out FLAT at 3.01/3.03/3.08/3.03 - a clean negative result that had the fix one sentence from being reverted. The instrument was sampling a unit's shot count from the driver loop, so **every shot taken on a unit's LAST action was invisible** (the turn ended inside a `later()` chain before the loop looked again), and `beginTurn` self-drives foe and ally turns, so the enemy-archer column read **0 shots across 220 turns** - impossible, and the tell. Rebuilt on the two real doors - **`beginTurn` opens the record, `strike` attributes to it** - the same A/B at n=112 per arm gives **blocked-lane dry turns 82 -> 17**, replicated **72 -> 22**; dry turns 47.3% -> 42.8%; shots per archer turn 0.99 -> 1.04. Win rate and rounds unmoved (119/128 vs 120/128, 6.8 vs 6.5 rounds) - those read `B.won`/`B.round` directly and were never touched by the attribution bug. ⚑ **THE LESSON IS NOT "n WAS TOO SMALL", IT IS THAT A NULL RESULT DESERVES THE SAME AUDIT AS A POSITIVE ONE.** #90's lesson was *n=6 cannot tell 0% from 20%*; this one is its other half - n was 128 and the answer was still wrong, because **a measurement that reports zero is not self-evidently honest**, and the 0-shots-in-220-turns column was the free tell that said so. ⚑ **WHAT THE CORRECTED INSTRUMENT FOUND THAT THE FIX DOES NOT ADDRESS**, and all three are written down rather than left to be rediscovered as ideas: **ENGAGED is the biggest bucket of all** (93 -> 119 dry turns; a bow is off the table while something stands on you, `aiTurn`'s kite rule is gated on `!engaged(u)` and **`autoStep` has no kite at all**, which is the two-brain divergence this file keeps being bitten by); **"had a >=20% shot and loosed nothing" is 26% of archer turns** and is mostly a turn spent entirely walking; and the lane fix **trades blocked for engaged** (blocked -50, engaged +26) because a hex with a clean lane is a more exposed hex. Net dry is down and shots are up, so the trade is worth taking, but it is a trade and it is not free. **#99 and 8f.127 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.128 | **#100 SHIPPED - THE BATTLE SCREEN, ROUND SIX: FOURTEEN POINTS, AND THREE OF THEM WERE ONE BOX MEASURED WRONG.** *(User, 2026-08-11: a fourth annotated battle shot, fourteen points, "commit and push after changes".)* ⛔ **THE SIXTH PASS ON THIS SURFACE IN A WEEK, AND THE SHAPE OF THE LIST IS AGAIN THE FINDING: nothing was asked to move back.** #91 built the frame, #94 and #95 finished it, #96 coloured it, #98 made the rail a queue, and this round is furniture: where four boxes sit, what two words mean, and one wash that was eating the top of the board. ⚑ **THE ONE REAL DIAGNOSIS IS POINT 11, AND THE COMPLAINT NAMED THE WRONG CULPRIT.** *"the border in turn order for unit that is moving feels a bit cutted from the right side - i want it to look nice with or without battle backlog"*: the log was innocent. `#bTop` is shrink-to-fit, so with `align-items:flex-start` it was **exactly 40px wide - one head** - while `.ord.now` draws a 2px ring OUTSIDE its border box and is pushed 3px right on top of that, and `overflow:hidden` (there to keep a long rail off the plaque) was cutting the ring's right-hand half off in every state, log or no log. **12px of padding is the ring, the shift and some of the glow**, and the clip stays because it is a guard rather than a layout. ⚑ **AND THE LOG STOPPED PRETENDING ONE LEFT EDGE FITS BOTH ITS STATES.** Shut it is a 150px button and belongs to the column of heads under it, so it takes their x (8, aligned as asked); open it is a panel that must not lie on the rail or its ring, so it steps to **64** - clear of the rail's padded box at 60 - and shrinks to **300x300** from 360x380. The left edge transitions with the width, so it is still visibly one object moving. ⚑ **THE RAIL IS ONE COLOUR FOR THE COMPANY NOW.** The gold left edge meant CAPTAIN, which put a second gold mark beside the gold ring that means *acting now*, to name the one body the board already marks with a ★. The company is teal, the ring is the only thing that singles anybody out, and **the gold goes to the body nobody could place: an ALLY** - on your side, in your half of the queue, and not yours to command. ⚑ **POINT 7 WAS ALREADY WRITTEN AND THE BATTLE SCREEN WAS THE ONE PLACE THAT DID NOT ASK FOR IT.** `RACEHELP`, `CLASSHELP` and `bindLoreTips` have explained both words on the character sheet since 8f.117; the plaque printed the same two words as flat text on the screen where somebody meets a spearwoman for the first time. **A wiring job, not a writing one, so the sheet and the plaque cannot drift.** ⚠ Bound on CHANGE, not per render: `bindLoreTips` builds a whole unit to list what is on the bar, and `render()` runs on every visible change, so it is keyed on the body being described and runs once a turn. ⚠ **And it is handed the LIVE unit** (`bindLoreTips(root,p,live)`), because left to build its own from the roster it re-rolls a caster's schools and lists spells that are not on the bar behind it. **The rest as asked:** the class line reads **Lv · class · race** · the armour bar names its weight, **LIGHT / MEDIUM / HEAVY off `armourMax`** and not off the item, so a brigand who was never handed a GEAR entry still gets an answer and a human in an ogre's plates is honestly reported as the lesser thing it has become on him (rags 6 to a door-plank 20 LIGHT · hides 26 to a jack 34 MEDIUM · mail 66 to ogre scale 104 HEAVY, and every one of those costs its wearer agility) · the queue **hangs off the top of its band** instead of centring in it, which is the *"huge space on top"* · **↻ II**, no word, the glyph the rail's own round divider has been drawing since #98 · **⚙ DEV.MODE** down a row and **♪ sound up** to join it, which gives FULL/FIELD/CLOSE back the corner #95 had to push it 96px out of. ⚑ **POINT 13 WAS A NUMBER NOBODY HAD EVER MEASURED AGAINST THE BOARD.** *"when character on the toppest tile, it is still good visible (right now it is a bit shadowed)"* - the vignette's top band was **fully opaque, fading out over 24% of 720px, which is 173px, four hex rows.** The board's top row occupies y15 to y83, so a body standing there was painted through .91 falling to .52. **.42 clearing by 8% (58px)** puts the same row at .31 falling to 0, most of it under .1. The bottom edge is untouched at .55: nothing stands below the board, and that end is sinking the ground under the card row, which is what a vignette is for. ⚠ **SIX COPIES OF THAT GRADIENT EXIST** - one in the sheet and one per battlefield inside `GROUND{}`, each in its own colour - and the one in the sheet is only what a battle sees before `drawTerrain()` runs. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `#cssTail` parsed · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve with `__errs` empty · **a fifteen-box overlap sweep clean in both log states with DEV.MODE ON**, nothing off-stage · the plaque rendered for every body in two fights (roster, captain, caster, ally, pet, whelp, great beast) with no throw · all three tooltips fired · the character sheet's own two still fire on the two-argument call. ⚠ **THE PANE CANNOT COMPOSITE, SO A TRANSITIONED PROPERTY IS STUCK AT ITS START VALUE**: the open log measured 150 wide for as long as anybody watched, and the height (untransitioned) was the tell. | 8f.129 | **#102 SHIPPED - THE BATTLE SCREEN, ROUND EIGHT: TWELVE POINTS, AND THE TWO THAT WERE NOT LAYOUT WERE BOTH A READOUT LYING.** *(User, 2026-08-11: a fifth annotated battle shot, three images, twelve points.)* ⛔ **SEVENTH PASS ON THIS SURFACE, AND FOR THE SECOND ROUND RUNNING NOTHING WAS ASKED TO MOVE BACK.** Ten of the twelve are furniture: where four controls sit, what one header says, how much air is inside a card. The two that are not furniture are the ones worth keeping, and they are the same bug wearing two coats: **a control that changed something and could not say what it had changed.** ⚑ **POINT 3 IS AN INVERTED READOUT, AND THAT IS WHAT "IT DOESNT WORK PROPERLY" MEANS.** `PACE` is a DURATION multiplier: higher means every beat lasts longer. The button's title said `PACE ×2.5`, which promises the fastest board in the file and delivers the slowest one, so pressing toward the bigger number gave less of what the bigger number says - and the only way to learn what a press had done was to watch the next blow and guess. The ladder is **declared in SPEEDS and converted once**: `SPEEDV=[0.7,1,1.25,1.5,1.75]`, `PACEV` derived through `PACE_NORM/s`, and the face carries the number. **×1 is the board the game already ships** (PACE 1.75), because *normal* has to be what everybody already has; **×0.7 is the user's "1.0, 30% slower"**, and it is the old top of the ladder finally wearing an honest label; ×1.75 is the raw authored timings, which is the floor, because below PACE 1 the animations run shorter than the durations they were tuned against and blows land over each other. ⚠ `gt_pace` is still written in PACE, so an existing save keeps the speed it had, and the click handler compares floats now - `1.75/1.5` does not terminate, so `indexOf` on a derived value was a bug waiting for its first non-terminating division. ⚑ **POINT 1 IS THE SAME SHAPE ON THE PLAQUE.** Nerve was the only measure on that panel drawn in a different language: armour and hitpoints are a labelled row over a sectioned bar, nerve was a bordered chip coloured edge to edge in the rung's own colour - hence *"green block"* - and **the one thing it could not tell you was how close the next rung was.** *Happy* and *Happy, one bad turn from Ok* were the same picture. It is the third row now, off the same `nerveFrac()` the ladder itself reads, so the word and the bar cannot disagree about which rung that is. ⚠ **The ticks are the LADDER's own gates (15/40/70/90) and not even sections**: `--seg` is the blows-left rule and nerve is not spent in blows, so an evenly sectioned bar would draw a scale this game does not have - the wide stretch between 40 and 70 is the real shape of *Ok*. ⚑ **POINT 12 IS THE README'S OWN RULE IN THE ONE PLACE THAT HAD NEVER OBEYED IT.** `k:'main'` is the GEAR SLOT every rostered body fills, so `ICON.main` was asked to draw a sword, a spear, a maul and a hunting bow with one glyph - and an archer's basic attack has been showing ⚔ since the table was written. **Test what the act DOES** (has it range), which is the same sentence that fixed `BLOCKED{}`, `walkable`, `a.range` and the dog that came out `race:'ratkin'`. CRIPPLING SHOT had no entry at all and fell through to the same generic arrow as the bow it is fired from; it is `⇸` now, an arrow stopped by a bar, **and the CRIPPLED badge on the target's head wears the same mark**, so the card and the body it made agree. ⚑ **POINT 4, THE ROLE LINE, IS DELIBERATELY NOT ON EVERY CARD.** The face answered *what does it cost* and *what does it do to them* and never *why would I press it* - which is fine for a sword and useless for KICK, whose whole point is that it moves a body, and for SPEAR WALL, which has no damage on its face because it does not attack. Those are the two the user named and they are the two the row was worst at. `ROLE{}` is keyed on the act exactly like `ICON{}`, so one table answers for the cards, both AI brains' sheets and any enemy act that ever reaches a card; a tag reading "damage" under ARMING SWORD would be the duplicate half of the 2026-08-10 report wearing a new coat. **THE FURNITURE:** BATTLELOG, not THE FIGHT (5) · the `?` off the log header and onto the plaque, into the slot the nerve chip vacated in the same edit, so it costs no height and lands beside the three measures it explains (7) · ♪ MUSIC under ⚙ DEV.MODE and ⏱ SPEED under that, one right-aligned column of settings, with the three tester buttons dropped 60px because developer chrome yields to player chrome (2, 3) · ⚑ WITHDRAW hard against the right edge with END TURN sliding to right:44, the two ends of one decision on one row (6) · the vignette's TOP BAND deleted outright (8) · the queue's sprites FITTED instead of scaled by one factor (9) · the card 84 → 82 with the glyph's top margin 10 → 3 (10) · resting on a queue head opens the board's own readout (11). ⚑ **POINT 8 IS WHAT A SECOND COMPLAINT ABOUT ONE THING MEANS.** #100 measured that wash and halved it, and the answer came back as the same complaint plus *"not sure about it purpose"* - which is the tell: it was never too heavy, **it had no job.** At the bottom a vignette sinks the ground under the card row and nothing stands below the board, so that end pays for itself; at the top there is a board, and the only thing it did was dim the bodies standing on it. Six copies, one in the sheet and one per battlefield in `GROUND{}`. ⚑ **POINT 9 IS ONE SCALE FOR SPRITES THAT ARE NOT ONE SHAPE.** `.78` was a single factor applied to bodies running from 33x45 to 48x25 to 70x54, so it was chosen for the widest and every tall body paid in dead air above its head - measured, a human drew 32x38 in a 40x46 box and a ratkin 32x30. Fitted at `min(44/w,44/h)` a person fills the box top to bottom. ⚠ **The width cap is 44 in a 40px box on purpose**: four pixels of bleed each side land on the empty margin every sprite here carries and `.ord` is `overflow:hidden`, so a wide body loses air rather than shoulder. A dog still does not reach the top, and that is honest - it is a dog. ⚠ **AND `.ord img` CARRIED `width:30px`, WHICH WOULD HAVE THROWN EVERY FITTED SIZE AWAY** - a stylesheet rule beats an HTML attribute, and the fit is written on the element's own `width`/`height`. ⛔ **THE BUILD BUG THIS TIME WAS A MISSING `</div>`, AND IT LOOKED LIKE A LAYOUT OPINION.** Moving the `?` chip inside `.pbars` left `#bPlq` unclosed, so `#bActions`, `#bUndo`, `#bEnd` and the whole log became CHILDREN OF THE PLAQUE and every `right:` on the battle screen resolved against a 262px box. Nothing threw, `__cssTail` was true, the console was clean, and END TURN simply sat at x=37. **The tell was `offsetParent`**, and it is the cheapest question to ask when a `position:absolute` box lands somewhere impossible: not *which rule moved it* but *what is it measuring against*. ⚠ **`#bTop` STOPPED TAKING THE POINTER** in the same edit. It is a 56px column with no background, so its overlap with the plaque was invisible AND ate the plaque's hovers - and the two boxes had already been overlapping by 5px since #100 with nobody noticing. Band pinned to `bottom:190` (470px against the 461 a nine-head queue needs) and `pointer-events` moved onto the heads, so a future overlap can never matter. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `#cssTail` parsed · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve · **a sixteen-box overlap sweep clean, with DEV.MODE ON, nothing off the 1280x720 stage**, the only two hits deliberate containment · the SPEED ladder cycled through all five steps with `--pace` following · the queue-head readout opened, placed itself at x64 beside the rail and closed on leave · WITHDRAW checked live, disabled and locked · the worst card in the file (two-line name + damage + role) measured against the cost band, which is why the card is 82 and not 80. ⚠ **THE ROLE LINE ATE NINE OF THE ELEVEN PIXELS THE TOP GAVE BACK**, so point 10's honest gain is **two pixels of board, not a hex row**. Said plainly rather than rounded up. **PICTURE: `shots/102_battle_screen.html`**, four full-stage panels. **#102 and 8f.129 spent.** | ✅ DONE |

Probably under charter armor, health, morale, and below everething else (basically switch it with this block) -In general i prefere visible more significant health and armor and mood then % to hit and dodge... Maybe instead of words you paint near one - a sword (to hit) and a feet (to dodge) and hower with description -When units move through the field - make it only 10% smaller, rigth now it is too significant reducment".)* ⛔ **EIGHTH PASS ON THIS SURFACE, AND FOR THE FIRST TIME THE LIST IS NOT MOSTLY FURNITURE: THREE OF THE EIGHT ARE FAULTS THAT HAVE BEEN SHIPPING FOR WEEKS AND WERE REPORTED AS TASTE.** The user wrote them as preferences - *delete this line*, *make the walker bigger*, *give the picked soldier a border* - and all three turned out to be a box or a constant that was simply wrong. ⚑ **"A BIG BLACK LINE THAT COVERS PART OF THE TOP TILE" IS A TEN-PIXEL COORDINATE DISAGREEMENT, AND IT WAS HALF OF A SECOND BUG.** `.hrow{margin-top:-10px}` is on **every** row including the first, so the grid's content starts ten board-pixels above its own box - and `#bTerrain` was pinned at `top:0`. The top ten pixels of the top rank of tiles were therefore standing on #bField's dark surround rather than on painted ground: at ×2.20 that is a **22px black band the width of the whole board with thirteen hex-tips cut out of it**, which is exactly the thing the user drew a line under. ⚠ **AND THE PAINTER HAS BEEN OFF ITS OWN TILES SINCE THE CANVAS WAS WRITTEN.** `hexCentre()` - the one function every puddle, tuft, stone and camp fire is placed from - returned `r*32+21` for a hex that centres at `r*32+11`, and `HEXOFF` was the geometric half-pitch **19** against an odd-row indent that render() writes inline as **27**. So every feature on every battlefield in the game has been ten pixels low, and every odd row eight pixels left, invisibly, because a puddle is a blob and nobody had ever asked the DOM where the tile was. `top:-10px` on the canvas plus 27/18.5 in hexCentre fixes the black line and the alignment in the same three numbers; **measured after, three probe hexes at (0,0), (7,1) and (14,12) agree with the live DOM to a tenth of a pixel on both axes.** ⚑ **"MAKE IT ONLY 10% SMALLER" IS #bFx SITTING OUTSIDE THE ZOOMED LAYER, WHICH IS DELIBERATE AND WAS NEVER PAID FOR.** The walking clone was measured in BOARD pixels and dropped into the effects layer, which carries no camera transform - so a body that walks was drawn at **1x while the body it left behind is drawn at the stop's zoom**: 61% of standing size at FULL, **45% at FIELD and 40% at CLOSE**. Every walk in the game was a body shrinking to a doll, crossing the ground and growing back, and nobody had chosen it. The standing sprite's own rect through `relPt` already carries the zoom AND the stage scale, so 90% of that is the ten per cent asked for; ⚠ **it is anchored by the FEET and not the head** - the old `-0.62 × hexSpan()` margin was tuned so a 34px human's top landed right, and scaling from there would have grown every body downward through the ground. Measured at FULL and CLOSE: ratio 0.900, foot line within 0.06px. ⚑ **"PROPER TOP BORDER FOR PICKED SOLDIER (AS THE OTHER THREE)" IS #100 POINT 11 AGAIN, ON THE OTHER EDGE.** That one read *"the border in turn order... feels a bit cutted from the right side"* and was answered with 12px of right-hand padding, because `.ord.now` draws its gold ring OUTSIDE its border box. **Nothing was done about the top - and the acting head is always the FIRST head in a queue whose band is `overflow:hidden`, so the ring's top edge has been clipped on every turn of every fight since the rail turned vertical.** Three sides of a frame and a gap where the fourth should be, which is what the user described in five words. 8px of padding-top; the ring now clears the clip by 6. ⚑ **THE PLAQUE IS THE OTHER WAY UP, AND THE ORDER OF A PANEL IS AN ARGUMENT.** Two percentages sat directly under the face in 15px display type with the pools below them in 10px mono, so **the loudest numbers on the panel were the two that change every time you point at a different tile, and the two that decide whether this body lives were the quietest.** The pools are the body's STATE; hit and dodge are a property of a shot that has not been aimed yet, and the aiming card prints both in full the moment there is a target. So armour / hitpoints / nerve go under the face with the count at **19px** (nerve's value is a WORD, so it takes the weight and not the size), `setBar` splits "34/60" into a loud 19px *what is left* and a quiet 10px *out of*, the hitpoint numeral takes the same `hpCol()` the bar and the token take, and ACTIONS + the two chips + the `?` become the bottom row. ⚑ **THE TWO MARKS ARE DRAWN AND NOT TYPED, AND THAT IS FORCED.** Everything else on this screen is a unicode glyph off `ICON{}` - and **there is no glyph for a foot in any font this game can count on**: 👣 exists only as an emoji, which Windows paints in full colour, a brown sticker in a panel drawn in three greys. ⚔ does exist and is used on the cards, but a typed sword beside a drawn foot sits at a different weight on a different baseline and the pair has to read as a pair. Both are inline SVG in `currentColor`, filled rather than stroked (at 13px a 1px stroke is a grey suggestion), and **both were verified by rasterising them to a canvas at true size and printing the alpha grid as text** - the only way to look at a 13px icon in a pane that composites no frames. ⚑ **THE CAMERA CAN BE TAKEN AND GIVES ITSELF BACK.** Drag the ground, or push the pointer into the outer 30px of the field; `CAM.free` holds the view where the player left it and is released **the moment the acting subject changes**, so looking around costs nothing and is never sticky - a camera the player has to hand back is one they forget they took, and the next body acts off-screen. ⚠ A press that travels more than 5px (12 on touch) stops being a click, cancelled in the capture phase, so none of the 195 hex buttons lost a press. ⚠ **AND THE RIM PUSH IS A CHAINED `setTimeout`, NOT `setInterval`**: measured in the hidden pane, an interval fires **0 times in 1200ms** at a 32ms period while setTimeout is merely floored and the harness's MessageChannel shim unclamps it - README §5's rAF rule has a second half nobody had written down. **THE REST AS ASKED:** cards 60x82 -> **72x86** with the name 8 -> 9px, the glyph 19 -> 21, damage 9 -> 10.5, role 7 -> 8, cost 8 -> 9 (⚠ the height had to move with the font or 82 stopped being the measured floor for the worst card in the file) · heads 40x46 -> **48x51**, with the fit square 44 -> 48 in the same edit, because leaving it would have handed the extra height straight back as dead air above every head · the rail starts at **74** instead of 60, 26px under the log · class 9.5 -> 11px and trait 11 -> 12.5px. ⚑ **`RAIL_MAX` STOPPED BEING A CONSTANT, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY'S ONE PIECE OF DEBT PAID.** It was 9, *"derived, not guessed"*, against a band whose height is set by `bottom:190px` - a number that means *how tall is the plaque*, a box at the other end of the screen, changed by four entries in two weeks. **Nothing ever failed when the two disagreed: the rail simply overflowed into its own clip, which looks like a design decision.** `railMax()` reads the live band now. ⚠ **The honest cost of the bigger heads is two of them: the queue shows 7 where it showed 9**, and `bottom` is 223 because the plaque's REAL ceiling is a two-line trait at 207, not the 191 that happened to be on screen. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME:** `__cssTail` true · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve · hexCentre vs the live DOM exact on both axes at three probes · the terrain now spans board -10..430 against a grid of -10..416 · walker ratio 0.900 with the feet planted, at FULL and at CLOSE · drag moves the camera and swallows its click while a plain press still clicks · the rim push panned 0 -> -210 (the clamp) in 400ms and stopped dead on pointerleave · the camera released itself on the next turn (free true -> false, subject ilka -> foe0) · both hovers fire and hide · the acting head's ring clears the clip by 6px · the legend popup lands inside the stage · **the Fen-Mother's worst-case row ("ARMOUR HEAVY 170/170") measures 119 of 241px.** **PICTURE: `shots/105_battle_after.html`.** ⚠ **OPEN, and it is the one that matters most this round: nobody has played it with a hand on a mouse.** The pane composites no frames, so how the rim push FEELS - 11px a tick every 32ms - is a judgement nobody here can make, and it is the first control in this file whose whole quality is its speed. ⚠ **OPEN: the walker is 90% by decree.** It reads as "slightly further away", which is a defensible reason to keep it, but the user asked for a number rather than an effect and may want 100%. One literal. **#105 and 8f.132 spent.** | ✅ DONE |
| **NEXT** | **The plan lives in [`00_PLAN_AND_BACKLOG.md`](00_PLAN_AND_BACKLOG.md). Nothing in this file is next.** | - |

## 8f.105 — the removed send block, kept whole for restore

*Here because 8f.105 removed a working feature to test a guess, and a guess that turns out wrong
must cost one paste to undo. This is the code exactly as it stood at 8f.104, in
`prototype/grimtoll_slice.html`, inside `drawNotes()`. Restoring it means putting all four pieces
back and deleting the `nSend` button and its handler.*

**1. The address, above `const POLL=[`:**

```js
const FEEDBACK_TO='dmytriyvihrov@gmail.com';
```

**2. The href, after `const L=own?letter():null;`:**

```js
/* built once so the length can be checked before the anchor is written */
const mailHref=(()=>{
  if(!L)return '';
  const full='mailto:'+FEEDBACK_TO+'?subject='+encodeURIComponent(L.subject)+
    '&body='+encodeURIComponent(L.body);
  if(full.length<=1900)return full;
  return 'mailto:'+FEEDBACK_TO+'?subject='+encodeURIComponent(L.subject)+
    '&body='+encodeURIComponent('GRIMTOLL, playtest notes'+
      (JOURNAL.who?' from '+JOURNAL.who:'')+
      '\n\nIt was too long to put in the link. It is all on my clipboard. Pasting it here:\n\n');
})();
```

**3. The markup, where the `nSend` button now sits:**

```js
'<a class="popt sendit big" id="nMail" href="'+esc(mailHref)+'">'+
  '<b>✉ SEND IT TO ME</b><i>opens your mail addressed to '+esc(FEEDBACK_TO)+
  ', with the answers already written · the journal goes on your clipboard in the '+
  'same click, paste it under the line</i></a>'+
'<p style="margin-top:11px">No mail on this machine? Copy the box and send it to <b>'+
esc(FEEDBACK_TO)+'</b> any way you like. '+
'It is plain text — nothing in it but what is on this page and what you typed.</p>'+
```

**4. The handler, where the `nSend` handler now sits:**

```js
const mail=$('nMail');
if(mail)mail.onclick=()=>{
  try{ta.focus();ta.select();document.execCommand('copy');
    if(navigator.clipboard&&navigator.clipboard.writeText)
      navigator.clipboard.writeText(ta.value).catch(()=>{});}catch(e){}
  const note=mail.querySelector('i');
  if(note)note.textContent='the journal is on your clipboard. Paste it under the line.';
};
```

**And one line inside `letter()`**, whose tail read
`'\n\n----------\nThe full journal is on my clipboard. Pasting it below.\n\n'`
before the clipboard started carrying both halves at once.
